# Parity matrix

Every capability in the Expedition manual, one row each, with what newjourney does
today and what closing the gap takes. The rows were extracted from the manual
(`research/raw/Expedition.txt`, not redistributed; see
[research/raw/README.md](../../research/raw/README.md)) in our own words, and checked
against the code on 2026-09-29. **Manual** gives the line range in that text.

**Status:** Done (a sailor can do it in the app today) · Partial (library-only,
computed but not shown, or a reduced form) · Missing · N/A (a Windows or vendor
artefact with no job to do in a web app).
**Class:** Build (code plus free data, doable in the PWA) · Bridge (needs Signal K or
a native helper, [needs.md](needs.md) D2/D4) · Licence (a paid licence or agreement,
[needs.md §2](needs.md#2-licences-subscriptions-and-agreements)) · Hardware (can be
built and fixture-tested, but not finished until it works on the device,
[needs.md §3](needs.md#3-hardware-to-build-against-and-to-verify)) · N/A.

The loop updates rows in the same commit as the code that changes them. The score is
never typed by hand: run `node scripts/parity-score.ts --areas`, and each
[report](reports/) quotes it. `src/parity-matrix.test.ts` keeps the table
well-formed.

PWA equivalent: named boat profiles. |

Types: Bearing, Boat length, Distance, Latitude, Longitude, Percentage, Sail, Time, Time span, Vector. Racing Bravo renames them automatically. |

## Charts

| ID | Feature | What it does | Manual | Status | Class | Gap / how to close | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-003 | S-57 ENC vector charts | Loads official S-57 vector chart cells and renders their objects selectively. | 31, 3119-3137 | Partial | Build | S-57 cells decode to features (lib/charts/s57.ts over iso8211.ts, in a worker), with their updates applied in sequence; drawn under the course (13.3). Checked on built cells; a real one waits on D8 (13.1b). | WGS-84, drawn in Mercator. Free from NOAA (USA) and LINZ (NZ); paid in Canada, Hong Kong and the South China Sea. Needs an ISO 8211 parser (.000 base cell plus .001… updates) and S-52 presentation rules. Objects can be queried from the right-click menu. |
| P1-004 | S-63 encrypted ENCs | Loads encrypted official ENC cells bought from chart distributors. | 32, 3124, 3139-3141 | Missing | Licence | Needs an IHO S-63 OEM key and a permit service; out of scope for open source. | Sources: ChartWorld, Admiralty AVCS. Needs an IHO S-63 OEM manufacturer key plus user permits tied to a hardware ID. This is hard to do legitimately in open source. |
| P1-005 | BSB/KAP raster charts (RNC) | Displays georeferenced scans of paper charts. | 33, 3143-3164 | Partial | Build | BSB/KAP charts decode in a worker (lib/charts/bsb.ts: header, palettes, run-length rows), are placed from their REF points (Mercator fit) or polynomials with the DTM shift, and draw under the ENC on the Route chart. Checked on charts built to the layout; a real RNC waits on D8 (13.8b). Still to do: clip to the PLY border, datum conversion beyond DTM, and tiling for charts past 4096 px (they are shrunk). | The header carries georeferencing (REF/PLY points or polynomial coefficients), update date, datum and depth datum. Not always WGS-84 or Mercator: Exp converts to WGS-84 when it can. NOAA is retiring its RNCs in favour of ENCs. Some raster charts are unofficial and sold only as an aid to navigation. |

Needs the C-MAP OEM SDK; 4D draws through OpenGL. |

Needs a Navionics SDK and key (a web SDK exists). |

Canvas toBlob to PNG, then share or download. Chart licences may restrict this for commercial charts. |

| P1-091 | Built-in world chart | Simple global vector base map used where no other chart covers. | 978-980 | Done | Build | Global OSM raster basemap (RouteScreen.tsx:48-76, ChartSurface.tsx:44-72). The OSM tile usage policy limits scale. | Natural Earth or GSHHG coastlines. Options are in P1-302 and P1-308. |
| P1-092 | ENC detail level: Base, Standard or All | Picks how much ENC content is drawn, following the IMO display categories. | 994-998, 3308-3392 | Done | Build | Route → ENC: Base, Standard or All, after S-52's display categories (Display Base always drawn; soundings under All) (lib/charts/s52.ts CATEGORY). | IMO A.817(19) App. 2. Base (not enough for safe navigation): high-water coastline, own safety contour, isolated dangers inside safe water, overhead hazards, buoys and beacons, traffic schemes, scale, range and orientation, depth and height units. Standard adds: drying line, aids to navigation, fairway and channel limits, conspicuous features, restricted areas, chart-scale boundaries, caution indicators. All adds: spot soundings, cables and pipelines, ferry routes, full danger and aid details, caution text, edition date, datum, variation, graticule, place names. The manual says to use All when in doubt. |

Still to do: soundings in Standard as an option. | S-52 Standard normally leaves them out. |

MK is a 2-character code that matches a mark named exactly that, or a name starting with it plus a space (e.g. "4Y NRYDM"). Codes RP/RS = round to port/starboard, PP/PS = pass, LP/LS = leave (2013 codes), GT = gate (treated as a plain sail-to mark because the gate details are unknown). The start line is set separately on the Start ribbon. |

| P1-009 | GRIB 1 and GRIB 2 decoding | Reads edition 1 and edition 2 GRIB weather files. | 42 | Partial | Build | GRIB2 in a worker (src/lib/weather/grib2.ts): simple, complex, spatial-differencing, JPEG 2000 (jpx.ts, checked against OpenJPEG), PNG and CCSDS packing, bitmaps, lat/lon grids. GRIB1 (grib1.ts): simple packing, NCEP Table 2 and ECMWF tables 128/140. Both into WeatherCube, cropped across 0/360. Still to do: projected (Lambert, polar) grids, GRIB1 second-order packing, and a check on real NCEP/ECMWF files (D8). | GRIB2 needs JPEG2000, PNG and CCSDS unpacking (data templates 5.40/5.41/5.42) as well as simple packing. GFS is free from NOMADS. |
| P1-010 | NetCDF ingest | Reads NetCDF gridded data such as ocean-current models. | 43, 1920-1922 | Missing | Build | Decode NetCDF server-side (xarray) or in the browser with h5wasm, into WeatherCube. | NetCDF-3 is simple. NetCDF-4 is HDF5 underneath, which in a browser needs a WASM library such as h5wasm. Clear All removes NetCDF data together with GRIB data. |
| P1-011 | Merging several GRIB datasets | Combines and displays several GRIB datasets at once, even when areas, resolutions or models overlap. | 44 | Partial | Build | Routing merges all ticked GRIB files with the download, finest grid first, then newer run (orderSources/stackSources). The chart still shows one source at a time: the newest ticked file, else the download. | This slice does not give the rules for which dataset wins where they overlap. A sensible default is that higher resolution beats lower and newer beats older. Needs design. |

"AIS" wind presumably means AIS meteorological messages. Needs spatial interpolation. |

## Tides & currents

| ID | Feature | What it does | Manual | Status | Class | Gap / how to close | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-201 | Tidetech currents | Drag a box on the chart to download tidal and ocean-current data from Tidetech. | 1961-1967 | Missing | Licence | Tidetech subscription. | Also from the right-click menu. |
| P1-209 | Tide stations and predictions | Lists loaded tide and current stations and shows height or stream predictions for one. | 51, 2049-2058, 2611-2613 | Partial | Build | Weather → Tides: the nearest NOAA harmonic stations (a pick of five), the tide now and which way, a curve from six hours ago to a day and a half ahead, and the highs and lows above MLLW, worked out on the device from constants kept once fetched (lib/tides/stations.ts, harmonic.ts, components/TideSheet.tsx); matches NOAA's highs and lows to the minute and the centimetre. Still to do: current stations in the same list (11.3), secondary stations. | Includes a separate Secondary tides list. Harmonic prediction from constituents; NOAA CO-OPS data is public. |
| P1-210 | Hong Kong current import | Imports Hong Kong Hydrographic Office current predictions from CSV. | 2060-2073 | Missing | Build | A CSV importer for Hong Kong current predictions. | Columns: Date,Time,Knots,Degree,Latitude(WGS84),Longitude(WGS84), e.g. "2016-07-19,00:00,0.10,59,22.46…,114.35…". Times are Hong Kong local; the user chooses this when prompted. |
| P1-293 | Current set and drift from instruments | Current speed and direction worked out from GPS versus heading and boat speed. | 3000-3009 | Done | Hardware | Set and drift from damped COG/SOG against heading plus leeway and boat speed, only below a set rate of turn (the instrument's, or from the heading). | Current = ground vector (COG, SOG) - water vector (HDG corrected for leeway, BSP). Set is the direction the current flows towards, the opposite convention to wind. Damp heavily. |
| P2-049 | Navionics dynamic tide/current icons | Animated tide and current icons on Navionics charts. | 3996–3999 | Partial | Build | Tidal streams draw an arrow and speed at each NOAA current station near the venue for the timeline's time (WeatherScreen wx-diamonds; screens.test.tsx). Still to do: tide-height icons on the chart. | Gotcha: they use wall-clock time, not the Expedition display time. |

## Optimal routing

| ID | Feature | What it does | Manual | Status | Class | Gap / how to close | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1-002 | Parallel multi-core computation | Spreads heavy work, especially optimal routing, across all CPU cores. | 18-23 | Partial | Build | Only one routing worker and one solve at a time (client.ts, worker.ts:215-242). Add a worker pool for sweeps. | Exp asks for Win10/11 and 16 GB or more of RAM. PWA equivalent: a pool of Web Workers sized by navigator.hardwareConcurrency. Phones have much less memory, so cap the grid and isochrone sizes. |
| P1-012 | Several optimum routes on screen | Keeps and draws several optimised routes together so they can be compared. | 57, 2133-2145 | Done | Build | The last five routes are kept and drawn together under the current one for comparison (store.ts keptRoutes, RouteScreen 'kept' layer, keptFC tests). | Each solution has its own colour, and the Results window compares them. |
| P1-211 | Wave speed corrections | Table of percentage speed factors by wave height (m) and wave angle to the boat. | 61, 2080-2085 | Done | Build | A wave speed table (height by angle to the boat, % of polar) loaded in Route SETTINGS and applied per heading in the kernel, with the wave direction now carried in the field (lookupPct, waveFactor). | Multiplies polar speed. The overview also mentions wave avoidance. Needs wave height and direction from GRIB (e.g. WW3). |
| P1-212 | Start routing now | Sets the routing start time to now. | 2086-2088 | Done | Build | Routes depart now by default (RouteScreen.tsx:401). |  |
| P1-213 | Isochrone or grid routing | Choice of two routing algorithms: isochrone (slightly better) or grid (for like-for-like comparison studies). | 56, 2090-2101 | Done | Build | Route SETTINGS → Algorithm: the isochrones, or a grid router (rhumb line or great circle) that the worker runs instead, within 1% of the isochrones on open water. Among islands the grid can be boxed in, and says so. | Both should give very similar results. Implementing both lets users cross-check. |
| P1-214 | Routing sensitivity display | Shows how sensitive the optimal route is. | 60, 62, 2104-2106 | Done | Build | A 10-minute sensitivity fill (RouteScreen.tsx:1214-1247, isochrone.ts:1950-1963). | Detailed in the routing chapters (later slice). |

| P2-195 | Tacks/gybes only at routing steps | Allows manoeuvres only at step boundaries. | 5741–5751 | Missing | Build | Kernel flag allowing manoeuvres only at step boundaries. | Not recommended. The isochronal solver may fail to reach the finish with it on. |
| P2-196 | Air-density polar adjustment | Corrects the polar for air density from GRIB pressure, temperature and dew point or humidity. | 5753–5761 | Done | Build | Route SETTINGS → Air: the polar reads TWS × √(ρ/ρref), ρ from the forecast's pressure, 2 m temperature and humidity (dry air without it), ρref = 1013.25 hPa, 15 °C, 80 % ≈ 1.219 kg/m³ (lib/weather/airDensity.ts); the TWS limits keep the real wind. Open-Meteo now fetches temperature and humidity; GRIB TMP and RH at 2 m. | Reference: 1013.25 hPa, 15 °C, 80% RH (≈1.219 kg/m³ by calculation). Uses dry-air density if there is no humidity data. Swell source priority: swell dir/period/height → primary wave dir/period + Hs → mean wave dir/period + Hs. Data e.g. NOAA WaveWatch III / GFS-Wave. |

Tested against a recorded stream and a mocked port; live use needs the boat. | Arrives over RS-232 serial, UDP or TCP. In a browser: Web Serial (Chromium desktop and Android only, not iOS) or a WebSocket bridge such as a Signal K server. Validate checksums. |
| P1-020 | NMEA 2000 input | Reads NMEA 2000 (CAN bus) data through a gateway. | 102 | Partial | Bridge | Decoded in the app from a Yacht Devices USB gateway in RAW mode over Web Serial, or through a Signal K server: heading, attitude, rate of turn, rudder, speed, depth, log, position, COG/SOG, GNSS fix, wind and environment, with fast packets reassembled (lib/instruments/n2k.ts). No Actisense binary (BST) yet; not tried on a live bus. | Needs a gateway (Actisense, Yacht Devices, Digital Yacht). A browser only gets it through a gateway that re-publishes over WebSocket (e.g. Signal K). |
| P1-021 | Proprietary race-instrument drivers | Drivers for the major proprietary racing instrument buses. | 87-92, 97, 99-100, 103-108, 110 | Missing | Bridge | Use Signal K server plugins; direct drivers would need vendor protocol specs. | A+T; B&G (Hercules, Hydra, GoFree, H390-H5000); Bravo; Cosworth; DMK; KVH Quadro; Nexus NX2 FDX; NKE; Ockam; Optimizer; Sailmon; Pixel sur Mer Exocet; Tacktick; Ventus; VSPARS. Each is its own driver. Many specs are under NDA. B&G GoFree offers a network API, other buses need raw serial or UDP. |
| P1-022 | Gateway devices (Yacht Devices, Digital Yacht) | Receives data from third-party NMEA gateways and Wi-Fi bridges. | 91, 111 | Partial | Bridge | Through a Signal K server with the gateway: the client reads its WebSocket deltas (lib/instruments/signalk.ts). Checked against a recorded stream, not a live boat. | These usually send NMEA 0183 over UDP or TCP. Browsers cannot open raw sockets, so a relay is needed. |
| P1-023 | Garmin USB GPS | Reads position from Garmin GPS units over USB. | 93 | Missing | Bridge | The Garmin USB protocol needs a native helper, or WebUSB (Chromium only). | Garmin's own USB protocol. WebUSB could work, but only in Chromium. |
| P1-026 | Laser rangefinder input | Takes range and bearing fixes from a laser rangefinder. | 96 | Missing | Bridge | Read the serial laser through Signal K or Web Serial, then feed Ping laser. | Used by Ping laser (P1-129) and by setting a start-line end with the laser (P1-171). Serial device. |
| P1-027 | External compasses, MOB devices and other sensors | Reads extra compasses, man-overboard transmitters and other sensors. | 112 | Missing | Bridge | Connect through Signal K. There is no sensor code beyond GPS. | Devices are not listed in this slice. |
| P1-046 | Calibration interface | Opens Expedition's own calibration functions (the theory is in P1-284 to P1-289). | 561-564 | Partial | Hardware | Setup → Calibration: offsets and loaded tables with clear; no per-parameter enable switches and no saving tables back out. | The manual prefers calibrating in the instrument system itself when possible. |
| P1-047 | Instrument connection pages | Pages for configuring each instrument connection. | 566-569 | Done | Bridge | Setup → Instruments: Signal K server address, connect and disconnect, live status, retrying with backoff, and a mixed-content warning (InstrumentsSection, lib/instruments/signalk.ts). | Ctrl-I. Which options appear depends on the instrument types chosen. |
| P1-048 | GPS source selection | Chooses which input supplies the GPS position. | 571-573 | Partial | Build | The position comes from the phone GPS, the simulator or the instruments, which turn the phone GPS off. Still to do: an explicit choice among GPS sources. | PWA sources could be the browser Geolocation API or an instrument feed. |
| P1-070 | GPS satellite status window | Shows the satellite constellation, fix mode and signal-to-noise per satellite. | 760-763 | Partial | Bridge | Setup → GPS shows the source, fix age, estimated error, and from NMEA the kind of fix, satellites in use, HDOP and altitude; no per-satellite sky view or signal strengths (needs GSV/GSA). | Needs NMEA GSV/GSA sentences. The browser Geolocation API does not expose satellite data. |
| P1-084 | TWA includes leeway | Expedition's TWA is measured to the boat's track through the water: centreline TWA plus leeway. | 931-944 | Partial | Hardware | TWA leaves out leeway (tactics.ts:396-397), and leewayFrom is never called (wind.ts:115-123). Needs a heel input. | If the instruments do not add leeway, Exp adds it. This is the convention designers' polars use (and Ockam and modern B&G), and it matters for VMG, laylines and routing. As a result Exp's TWA reads a few degrees wider than the instruments'. |
| P1-235 | Reset trip logs | Zeroes the "Log Bsp" and "Log Sog" distance counters. | 2243-2245 | Missing | Build | Integrate SOG into a resettable trip log. A BSP log needs instruments. | Each is the time integral of BSP or SOG. |
| P1-284 | Boat speed calibration table | Speed-log calibration that varies with speed. | 2859-2862, 2896-2903 | Done | Hardware | An additive BSP table by boat speed and heel, loaded from a text grid in Setup → Calibration and applied before damping (lib/instruments/calibration.ts). | Paddlewheels become non-linear at speed; sonic sensors are more linear. Interpolate a factor-vs-BSP table. |
| P1-285 | Compass swing and heading offset | Deviation learned by swinging the compass, plus a fixed alignment offset so north reads 0°. | 2864-2872 | Done | Hardware | A compass offset, and a deviation table by magnetic heading interpolated round the compass (Setup → Calibration). No swing wizard to learn one. | The swing (steady circles under power) is done inside the compass; Exp or the PWA applies the linear offset. |
| P1-286 | Wind vane offset and other sensors | Fixes a misaligned masthead AWA sensor and calibrates depth, temperature and other sensors. | 2874-2883 | Missing | Hardware | AWA and depth offsets need the sensors. | AWS is normally factory-calibrated. Depth needs an offset to waterline or keel. |
| P1-287 | TWA correction table | Table by TWA and TWS that corrects true wind angle for upwash and mast twist. | 2905-2931 | Done | Hardware | An additive TWA table by TWA and TWS, applied with the side's sign after true wind is worked out, and TWD recomputed from it. | Sail the same TWA on each tack and compare TWD. Port TWA is negative internally. If TWD reads higher on port than on starboard, add half the difference to TWA; if higher on starboard, subtract half. |
| P1-288 | TWS correction table | Table by TWA and TWS that corrects the extra wind speed read downwind. | 2933-2943 | Missing | Hardware | A TWS table by TWA and TWS, applied to instrument wind. | Upwash makes TWS read high downwind. B&G subtracts a downwind amount that shrinks as TWA narrows; Exp and Ockam T1 use a 2-D TWA × TWS table. |
| P1-289 | Corrected apparent wind | Works corrected AWA and AWS back out from the corrected true wind. | 2945-2951 | Partial | Hardware | trueToApparent exists but is unused (wind.ts:47-76). Needs calibrated inputs. | Reverse wind triangle using corrected TWA/TWS and BSP (and leeway). |
| P1-290 | True wind from apparent wind | Computes TWS and TWA from apparent wind and boat speed. | 2959-2970 | Done | Hardware | The instrument pipeline works TWA, TWS and TWD out of damped AWA/AWS, boat speed and the water course, or takes the instruments' own, as chosen in Setup → Instruments (lib/instruments/pipeline.ts). | TWS = √(AWS² + BSP² - 2·AWS·BSP·cos AWA); TWA = atan2(AWS·sin AWA, AWS·cos AWA - BSP). Then add leeway (P1-084). |
| P1-291 | Leeway | Estimates sideways slip from heel and boat speed. | 2972-2991 | Done | Hardware | Leeway = k·heel/bsp² in the pipeline, on the course and in its own channel; k set in Setup → Instruments. | Leeway = -k·heel / BSP². Heel is negative to port, which gives positive leeway. k is a user coefficient (its value is set elsewhere). Guard against BSP near 0. |
| P1-292 | True wind direction | Wind direction over the water, from heading plus TWA. | 2993-2998 | Done | Hardware | TWD from the water course plus TWA, calculated from instruments in the pipeline, or heading plus the instruments' TWA. | TWD = HDG + TWA (mod 360), where the wind comes from. Needs a compass; variation gives true or magnetic. Keep the leeway sign convention consistent. |
| P1-295 | Extra sensor channels | Accepts heel, trim, load cell, sea temperature, barometer and air temperature alongside the five core sensors. | 3039-3106 | Partial | Bridge | Heel, trim, sea and air temperature, barometer, humidity, depth, rudder, mast and logs arrive from Signal K or serial as channels, and a flat-mounted phone's tilt gives heel; no load cells. | The core five are wind, depth, compass, speed and GPS. GPS also provides accurate time. Load cells are e.g. forestay tension. |
| P3-054 | Kill instrument start timer at gun | Stops instrument-side start/race timers at the gun where the system supports it. | 7517-7519 | Missing | Hardware | Send a timer-kill command over the instrument link (Signal K or vendor output). | Needs a two-way instrument link. |
| P3-061 | Calibration manager (enable, load/save, raw channels) | Per-parameter calibration with enable checkboxes, single values or tables, saved as editable text files. | 7592-7597, 7640-7668 | Partial | Build | Setup → Calibration: offsets and loaded tables with clear; no per-parameter enable switches and no saving tables back out. | Files in the data folder's config folder. Raw Bsp, Twa and Tws are kept in separate logged channels. Rate of turn and acceleration are always on (needed for starts). |
| P3-062 | Calibration table interpolation mode | Tables are interpolated with a smooth spline by default, with linear as an option. | 7669-7670 | Partial | Build | Calibration tables interpolate bilinearly, held at the edges (calibration.ts). Still to do: the spline default and the choice. | Spline type not specified; a natural cubic spline is a reasonable match. |

Tip: tune until start-display turn circles match turns seen in the track. Most important start calibration. |

Measure by slowing and timing acceleration in Stripchart. No acceleration assumed beyond polar speed. |
| P3-065 | Braking calibration table | Seconds to stop as a function of rate of turn. | 7614-7619, 7705-7708 | Missing | Build | Add braking table; use in the turn-accelerate-sail dynamics in startline.ts. | Optional, default off. |

Roll positive to starboard (i.e. sailing on port); bow-up pitch positive. |

| P3-068 | Boat speed calibration table | Additive boat-speed corrections by speed and optionally heel. | 7731-7735 | Done | Hardware | An additive BSP table by boat speed and heel, loaded from a text grid in Setup → Calibration and applied before damping (lib/instruments/calibration.ts). | Bsp across, heel down; usually one row. |
| P3-069 | Apparent wind speed calibration | Additive AWS correction table by AWA and AWS. | 7737-7742 | Done | Hardware | An additive AWS table by AWA and AWS, from a text grid in Setup → Calibration. | AWA across, AWS down. aws = cal + awsraw. Advanced only. |
| P3-070 | True wind angle and speed calibration tables | Additive TWA and TWS corrections by TWA and TWS, laid out like a polar file. | 7744-7756 | Partial | Hardware | TWA and TWS tables by TWA and TWS work, but load from our own text grid, not Expedition's polar-file layout. | TWA across, TWS down. TWS values are usually negative. Default layout mirrors the Expedition polar format. |
| P3-071 | Heading calibration | Compass offset as a single value or a heading-dependent table. | 7758-7762 | Done | Build | A compass offset, and a deviation table by magnetic heading interpolated round the compass (Setup → Calibration). No swing wizard to learn one. |  |
| P3-072 | Leeway coefficient calibration | Leeway estimated from heel and boat speed using a coefficient k, single or tabled by TWA/TWS. | 7764-7789 | Partial | Build | Leeway from heel and boat speed with one k (pipeline.ts); no k table by TWA/TWS and no leeway from speed components. | Leeway = k * heel / bsp^2 (Ockam/B&G style). Typical k 10–12, biased to upwind. Table form gives k per TWA (across) / TWS (down); can be fitted from a designer VPP. Heel and leeway positive on port. |

Pitch: bow up +. Roll: + to starboard (sailing on port). Leeway: clockwise +, so > 0 on port; course = heading + leeway; leeway ~ k*heel/bsp^2. |

Web Serial could cover this on desktop Chromium only. |

The meaning can depend on the type letter. The source table is garbled, and the manual shows "T" for both RPM and temperature, whereas the XDR standard uses C for temperature and T for tachometer, so accept both. |

Impossible in a browser without a native bridge. |

No DSC; Signal K and NMEA 2000 AIS not read yet. | AIS arrives as VDM/VDO sentences; AIS-SART MMSIs start with 970; DSC comes as DSC/DSE sentences. Needs CPA/TCPA. Settings are in a later slice. |

| P3-143 | Expedition protocol sentence framing and checksum | Plain-text lines starting with # and ending with *XX CRLF carrying boat id and var/value pairs. | 9443-9469 | Partial | Hardware | Codec with the #-inclusive XOR checksum, data lines kept as channel numbers, commands parsed, ACK built (lib/instruments/expProtocol.ts), received on the serial port. Naming data channels needs Expedition's numbering; sending ACKs waits on D3. | Data: `#<boat>,<var>,<val>,<var>,<val>...*XX\r\n`. External channels: `E,var,val,...`; system channels (held wind/current): `X,var,val,...`. XX = 2-hex-digit XOR of every character from `#` up to (not incl.) `*` (verified: `#G,12.34` gives 62). Replies `#ACK,<checksum of command>*XX`. Alternating channels send the mapped channel id as value (Sailmon: the value). Ideal for a WebSocket bridge. |

Alternating channels effectively double display slots (e.g. target Bsp + target TWA on Alternating 0 sent to CFD). |

Options: channel mapping (incl. alternating), damped, disable timeout for irregular data, custom tag names, refresh tags (10 s), alternating tag names (heavy bus load). Each display updates at most 5 Hz; 10 displays is about 1 Hz. If CAN load < 40% it may send faster, only where load is known (Actisense NGT-1, NGX-1, Digital Yacht iKonvert). |

Writing waits on D3. | NMEA is RS-422 electrically, so use an opto-isolated or RS-422→RS-232 adaptor. DB9 pins: 2 = RX+ (from device out+), 3 = TX+ (to device in+), 5 = ground. Expedition sets every PGN to receive. |

## Appendix: corrections to channels-reference.md

Expedition's Appendix A lists 314 entries, or 361 channels per boat once the numbered
ranges are expanded (Alternating 0–9, User 0–31, Polar 5–8).
[channels-reference.md](../01-expedition-analysis/channels-reference.md) names almost
all of them. These are the entries it gets wrong or leaves implicit. Worklist item 2.1
builds the channel catalogue from the appendix itself.

| Channel | Meaning (own words) | Manual line | Missing or wrong? |
|---|---|---|---|
| (whole catalogue count) | The appendix lists 314 entries (361 with ranges expanded). | 12993-13785 | **Wrong.** The reference says "roughly 400" (line 3) and "Cutting the ~400" (line 228). |
| Layline tide port side port time | First-leg time on the tide-aware route to the port side of the course. | 13247 | **Missing by name.** The reference only says "`Layline tide …` (6 tide-corrected variants)". |
| Layline tide port side stbd time | Second-leg time on the tide-aware port-side route. | 13248 | **Missing by name** (same ellipsis). |
| Layline tide stbd side port time | First-leg time on the tide-aware starboard-side route. | 13249 | **Missing by name.** |
| Layline tide stbd side stbd time | Second-leg time on the tide-aware starboard-side route. | 13250 | **Missing by name.** |
| Layline tide port side time | Total tide-aware time to the mark via the port side. | 13251 | **Missing by name.** |
| Layline tide stbd side time | Total tide-aware time to the mark via the starboard side. | 13252 | **Missing by name.** The display-settings text (12053-12065) that explains these six is internally inconsistent, so keep leg1/leg2/total per side. |
| Tide left time | Total time via the tide-affected left-hand layline route. | 13668 | **Missing.** The reference shorthand "`Tide left/right (port/stbd) time`" covers only the per-tack variants, not the totals. |
| Tide right time | Total time via the tide-affected right-hand layline route. | 13671 | **Missing** (same shorthand gap). The Tide left/right set may duplicate the Layline tide set under older names; worth checking. |
| Awa, Aws, Bsp, Current drift, Current set, Depth, Heading, Leeway, Twa, Twd, Tws | When chosen in calibration, each carries Expedition's own calculated and calibrated value instead of the raw instrument value. | 13008-13013, 13026-13028, 13046-13052, 13067-13069, 13184-13186, 13272-13275, 13695-13704, 13719-13721 | **Missing behaviour.** The reference treats these as plain sensor values, with no raw-vs-calibrated switch. |
| Twa (leeway handling) | If the instruments give TWA to the centreline, Expedition adds leeway to it. | 13695-13701 | **Partly missing.** The reference keeps "includes leeway" but not the rule that leeway is added to centreline TWA. |
| Twd from targets | The appendix gives no definition. | 13708 | **Unsourced.** The reference's definition ("TWD implied by sailing at target TWA") is actually the appendix's definition of `Target Twd` (13650-13655), so it may be a duplicate or misattributed. |
| Twd twist | The appendix gives no definition. | 13718 | **Unsourced.** "Wind shear/twist with height" is a guess. |
| Motion | The appendix gives no definition. | 13323 | **Unsourced grouping.** The reference ties it to the Volvo wave sensor, but the appendix links only `Slam` (13471-13473) and the four `Wave …` channels (13780-13785) to that sensor. |
| Sail AI stripe channels (Lower/Mid/Upper Stripe × Camber/Draft/Twist/Entry/Exit) | Sail-stripe shape at 25/50/75% of P. | 13415-13458 | **Unsourced attribution.** The reference calls them "VSPARS-class optical" and camera-based, but the appendix names only "Sail AI" and no vendor or sensor. |
| Rig/load/engine group (e.g. Rudder, RPM1/2, Voltage, Forestay load/length, Runner port/starboard, MainSheet, Traveller, Keel Angle, Mast angle, Board, Downhaul load) | Rig, foil, load and engine sensor values. | 13078-13082, 13098-13102 | **Wrong.** The reference says "All require NMEA 2000 hardware" (line 217). The appendix says the downhaul and forestay loads map from B&G linear channels. The NMEA 0183 section decodes most of this group via XDR/RSA/RPM (10742-10846), and Ockam aux channels also feed it (11247-11250). |
| Engine temperature, Engine oil pressure | Also flagged red in number boxes on NMEA 2000 over-temperature or low-oil-pressure warnings. | 13084-13090 | **Missing behaviour.** |
| CAN fast packet errors | N2K fast-packet error count, sourced from Actisense or a B&G WebSocket. | 13031-13032 | **Missing source detail.** The B&G WebSocket source is not mentioned anywhere in the reference. |
| User 0–31 | Custom channels with reservations: Ockam calibration values in User 10–18, Ockam user tags in User 0–8, and some renamed by Dfw2Exp. | 13727-13733 | **Missing detail.** Allocation conflicts matter if we copy the numbering. |
| Gate bias length | Distance bias of the gate for the current wind; port/starboard sense is taken from the previous mark. | 13138-13141 | **Missing detail** (the sign convention). |
| Port gate lay dst on strb / Port gate lay tm on strb | Distance/time to the *port* layline of the port gate mark. | 13390-13393 | **Missing warning.** Same naming trap as `Layline distance on port`, but the reference only flags the layline case. |
| Port gate lay dst on pt / Port gate lay tm on pt | Distance/time to the *starboard* layline of the port gate mark. | 13394-13398 | **Missing warning** (same trap). |
| Start gun dist below line | Signed distance below (or above) the line at the gun. | 13508-13509 | **Imprecise.** The reference says "Predicted position at the gun", but the channel is a signed distance, not a position. |
| Start boat to pin time | Time from the boat's current position to reach the pin end along the line. | 13493-13494 | **Imprecise.** "Time to run the line" suggests an end-to-end run; the channel is boat→pin. |
| GWD / GWS | Wind relative to the earth; TWD/TWS are relative to the water. | 13176-13183 | **Ambiguous.** "GW = TW + current" holds only for velocity vectors in one consistent "towards" convention (wind is quoted "from", current "towards"). Also, the appendix's TWS wording ("with regard to current") looks like a manual typo for "without". |
| Polar 5, 6, 7, 8, 5%, 6%, 7%, 8% | Extra user-defined polars and their percentages. | 13372-13373 | **Unsourced example.** The reference's "(e.g. keel angle)" is not in the appendix (minor). |
